But the mu tile index we store into is still corresponding to original gx_mu. This effectively duplicates boundary mu into outside halo. Good. The x dimension is a full warp (32 contiguous doubles) so\n// every warp performs coalesced global/shared accesses.\nconstexpr int BLOCK_X = 32;\nconstexpr int BLOCK_Y = 4;\nconstexpr int BLOCK_Z = 4;\nconstexpr int BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y * BLOCK_Z;\n\n// Concentration tile has a two-cell halo because it is used to compute the\n// chemical potential one cell outside the block (which is needed by the\n// concentration update).\nconstexpr int C_TILE_X = BLOCK_X + 4;\nconstexpr int C_TILE_Y = BLOCK_Y + 4;\nconstexpr int C_TILE_Z = BLOCK_Z + 4;\nconstexpr int C_TILE_XY = C_TILE_X * C_TILE_Y;\nconstexpr int C_TILE_SIZE = C_TILE_XY * C_TILE_Z;\n\n// Chemical-potential tile has a one-cell halo.\nconstexpr int MU_TILE_X = BLOCK_X + 2;\nconstexpr int MU_TILE_Y = BLOCK_Y + 2;\nconstexpr int MU_TILE_Z = BLOCK_Z + 2;\nconstexpr int MU_TILE_XY = MU_TILE_X * MU_TILE_Y;\nconstexpr int MU_TILE_SIZE = MU_TILE_XY * MU_TILE_Z;\n\n__device__ __forceinline__ int clampIndex(int v, int n) {\n    return v < 0 ? 0 : (v >= n ? n - 1 : v);\n}\n\n// Initialize the concentration field exactly like the reference implementation.\n__global__ void initializeConcentrationKernel(double* __restrict__ c, size_t vol) {\n    const size_t i = static_cast<size_t>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (i >= vol) {\n        return;\n    }\n    const double pseudo =\n        ((((i + 1) * static_cast<size_t>(1299709)) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n    c[i] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n}\n\n// One complete Cahn-Hilliard time step:\n//   mu  = f(c) - gamma * laplacian(c)\n//   c   = c + dt * D * laplacian(mu)\n// Both stencils are fused into a single kernel. The concentration tile\n// (two-cell halo) is staged in shared memory, the chemical potential is\n// computed into a one-cell-halo shared tile, and the concentration update is\n// then computed from that shared tile. This removes the global-memory\n// round-trip of the mu array and halves the number of kernel launches.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE, 2) void cahnHilliardStepKernel(\n    const double* __restrict__ cold, double* __restrict__ cnew, int nx, int ny, int nz,\n    double invdx2, double invdy2, double invdz2, double gamma, double e_AA, double e_BB,\n    double e_AB, double dtD) {\n    __shared__ double c_tile[C_TILE_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double mu_tile[MU_TILE_SIZE];\n\n    const int bx0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BLOCK_X;\n    const int by0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BLOCK_Y;\n    const int bz0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * BLOCK_Z;\n    const int tid = threadIdx.x + threadIdx.y * BLOCK_X + threadIdx.z * BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y;\n\n    // Stage concentration values including the two-cell halo, applying the\n    // same clamped boundary conditions as the reference code.\n    for (int i = tid; i < C_TILE_SIZE; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int cz = i / C_TILE_XY;\n        const int r = i - cz * C_TILE_XY;\n        const int cy = r / C_TILE_X;\n        const int cx = r - cy * C_TILE_X;\n\n        const int gx = clampIndex(bx0 + cx - 2, nx);\n        const int gy = clampIndex(by0 + cy - 2, ny);\n        const int gz = clampIndex(bz0 + cz - 2, nz);\n        c_tile[i] = cold[(static_cast<size_t>(gz) * ny + gy) * nx + gx];\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    // Compute the chemical potential for the block interior plus a one-cell\n    // halo. For halo positions outside the domain the center is clamped, which\n    // reproduces the reference clamped-boundary semantics.\n    for (int i = tid; i < MU_TILE_SIZE; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int mz = i / MU_TILE_XY;\n        const int r = i - mz * MU_TILE_XY;\n        const int my = r / MU_TILE_X;\n        const int mx = r - my * MU_TILE_X;\n\n        const int cxg = clampIndex(bx0 + mx - 1, nx);\n        const int cyg = clampIndex(by0 + my - 1, ny);\n        const int czg = clampIndex(bz0 + mz - 1, nz);\n\n        const int ccx = cxg - bx0 + 2;\n        const int ccy = cyg - by0 + 2;\n        const int ccz = czg - bz0 + 2;\n        const int ci = (ccz * C_TILE_Y + ccy) * C_TILE_X + ccx;\n\n        const double cv = c_tile[ci];\n        const double cxx = (c_tile[ci + 1] + c_tile[ci - 1] - 2.0 * cv) * invdx2;\n        const double cyy = (c_tile[ci + C_TILE_X] + c_tile[ci - C_TILE_X] - 2.0 * cv) * invdy2;\n        const double czz = (c_tile[ci + C_TILE_XY] + c_tile[ci - C_TILE_XY] - 2.0 * cv) * invdz2;\n\n        mu_tile[i] = 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB) +\n                     3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv - gamma * (cxx + cyy + czz);\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    const int x = bx0 + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = by0 + threadIdx.y;\n    const int z = bz0 + threadIdx.z;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny || z >= nz) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    // The mu tile is indexed so that index (mx,my,mz) corresponds to the\n    // physical cell (bx0+mx-1, by0+my-1, bz0+mz-1). The update thread needs\n    // the cell itself as the stencil center.\n    const int mi = ((threadIdx.z + 1) * MU_TILE_Y + (threadIdx.y + 1)) * MU_TILE_X +\n                   (threadIdx.x + 1);\n    const double muc = mu_tile[mi];\n    const double mxx = (mu_tile[mi + 1] + mu_tile[mi - 1] - 2.0 * muc) * invdx2;\n    const double myy = (mu_tile[mi + MU_TILE_X] + mu_tile[mi - MU_TILE_X] - 2.0 * muc) * invdy2;\n    const double mzz =\n        (mu_tile[mi + MU_TILE_XY] + mu_tile[mi - MU_TILE_XY] - 2.0 * muc) * invdz2;\n\n    const size_t idx = (static_cast<size_t>(z) * ny + y) * nx + x;\n    cnew[idx] = cold[idx] + dtD * (mxx + myy + mzz);\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx,\n                    [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Check if values are in reasonable range for concentration field\n    // After Cahn-Hilliard evolution, values should typically remain bounded\n    double minVal = c[0];\n    double maxVal = c[0];\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Concentration range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // Values should generally stay within reasonable bounds\n    if (maxVal > 10.0 || minVal < -10.0) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 64)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n printf(\"Grid size: %zu x %zu x %zu\\n\", nx, ny, nz);\n    printf(\"Time steps: %d\\n\", iterations);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n\n    // Physical static_cast<unsigned int>((nz + BLOCK_Z - 1) / BLOCK_Z));\n\n    // Run simulation\n    printf(\"Running Cahn-Hilliard simulation...\\n\");\n    auto start = Find results block\n    match = re.search(r'=== RESULTS ===(.*?)=== END RESULTS ===', text, re.DOTALL)\n    if not match:\n        return None\n    \n    block = match.group(1)\n    \n    # Parse fields\n    name_match = re.search(r'Name:\\s*(\\S+)', block)\n    elements_match = re.search(r'Elements:\\s*(\\d+)', block)\n    sum_match = re.search(r'Sum:\\s*([^\\n]+)', block)\n    min_match = re.search(r'Min:\\s*([^\\n]+)', block)\n    max_match = re.search(r'Max:\\s*([^\\n]+)', block)\n    hash_match = re.search(r'Hash:\\s*([0-9a-fA-F]+)', block)\n    \n    if not name_match or not elements_match or not hash_match:\n        return None\n    \n    # Parse samples\n    samples = {}\n    for sample_match in re.finditer(r'Sample\\[(\\d+)\\]:\\s*([^\\n]+)', block):\n        idx = int(sample_match.group(1))\n        val_str = sample_match.group(2).strip()\n        try:\n            samples[idx] = float(val_str)\n        except ValueError:\n            # Integer sample\n            samples[idx] = float(int(val_str))\n    \n    # Determine if integer-based (no Sum field or integer Min/Max)\n    is_integer = sum_match is None\n    \n    sum_val = None\n    min_val = None\n    max_val = None\n    \n    if sum_match:\n        try:\n            sum_val = float(sum_match.group(1).strip())\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n    \n    if min_match:\n        try:\n            min_val = float(min_match.group(1).strip())\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n    \n    if max_match:\n        try:\n            max_val = float(max_match.group(1).strip())\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n    \n    return Results(\n        name=name_match.group(1),\n        elements=int(elements_match.group(1)),\n        sum_val=sum_val,\n        min_val=min_val,\n        max_val=max_val,\n        samples=samples,\n        hash_val=hash_match.group(1).lower(),\n        is_integer=is_integer\n    )\n\n\ndef compare_results(\n    baseline: Results,\n    test: Results,\n    tolerance: float = 1e-10,\n    gpu_tolerance: float = 1e-6,\n    is_gpu: bool = False\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compare test results against baseline.\n    \n    Returns a dict with:\n    - correct: bool\n    - max_rel_error: float\n    - failed_samples: list of dicts with index, expected, actual, rel_error\n    - details: str with human-readable summary\n    \"\"\"\n    result = {\n        'correct': True,\n        'max_rel_error': 0.0,\n        'failed_samples': [],\n        'details': ''\n    }\n    \n    tol = gpu_tolerance if is_gpu else tolerance\n    errors = []\n    \n    # Check element count\n    if baseline.elements != test.elements:\n        result['correct'] = False\n        errors.append(f\"Element count mismatch: expected {baseline.elements}, got {test.elements}\")\n    \n    # For integer data, compare hashes exactly\n    if baseline.is_integer:\n        if baseline.hash_val != test.hash_val:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            errors.append(f\"Hash mismatch: expected {baseline.hash_val}, got {test.hash_val}\")\n        result['details'] = '\\n'.join(errors) if errors else \"Hash match: OK\"\n        return result\n    \n    # For floating-point data, compare samples with tolerance\n    def rel_error(expected: float, actual: float) -> float:\n        if abs(expected) < 1e-15:\n            return abs(actual)\n        return abs(actual - expected) / abs(expected)\n    \n    # Compare sum\n    if baseline.sum_val is not None and test.sum_val is not None:\n        err = rel_error(baseline.sum_val, test.sum_val)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        if err > tol:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            errors.append(f\"Sum mismatch: expected {baseline.sum_val:.17e}, got {test.sum_val:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    # Compare min/max\n    if baseline.min_val is not None and test.min_val is not None:\n        err = rel_error(baseline.min_val, test.min_val)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        if err > tol:\n            errors.append(f\"Min mismatch: expected {baseline.min_val:.17e}, got {test.min_val:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    if baseline.max_val is not None and test.max_val is not None:\n        err = rel_error(baseline.max_val, test.max_val)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        if err > tol:\n            errors.append(f\"Max mismatch: expected {baseline.max_val:.17e}, got {test.max_val:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    # Compare samples\n    for idx in baseline.samples:\n        if idx not in test.samples:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            errors.append(f\"Missing sample at index {idx}\")\n            continue\n        \n        expected = baseline.samples[idx]\n        actual = test.samples[idx]\n        err = rel_error(expected, actual)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        \n        if err > tol:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            result['failed_samples'].append({\n                'index': idx,\n                'expected': expected,\n                'actual': actual,\n                'rel_error': err\n            })\n            errors.append(f\"Sample[{idx}] mismatch: expected {expected:.17e}, got {actual:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    result['details'] = '\\n'.join(errors) if errors else f\"All comparisons passed (max_rel_error={result['max_rel_error']:.2e})\"\n    return result\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Compare benchmark results against baseline')\n    parser.add_argument('baseline', type=Path, help='Baseline output file or stdout capture')\n    parser.add_argument('test', type=Path, help='Test output file or stdout capture')\n    parser.add_argument('--tolerance', '-t', type=float, default=1e-10,\n                        help='Relative tolerance for floating-point comparison (default: 1e-10)')\n    parser.add_argument('--gpu-tolerance', type=float, default=1e-6,\n                        help='Tolerance for GPU results (default: 1e-6)')\n    parser.add_argument('--gpu', action='store_true',\n                        help='Use GPU tolerance (for CUDA/hybrid tiers)')\n    parser.add_argument('--json', action='store_true',\n                        help='Output result as JSON')\n    \n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    \n    # Read files\n    baseline_text = args.baseline.read_text()\n    test_text = args.test.read_text()\n    \n    # Parse results\n    baseline_results = parse_results(baseline_text)\n    test_results = parse_results(test_text)\n    \n    if baseline_results is None:\n        print(\"ERROR: Could not parse baseline results\", file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(2)\n    \n    if test_results is None:\n        print(\"ERROR: Could not parse test results\", file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(2)\n    \n    # Compare\n    comparison = compare_results(\n        baseline_results,\n        test_results,\n        tolerance=args.tolerance,\n        gpu_tolerance=args.gpu_tolerance,\n        is_gpu=args.gpu\n    )\n    \n    if args.json:\n        import json\n","metadata":{"output":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nCompare benchmark results against a baseline for validation.\n\nParses the standardized results output format:\n=== RESULTS ===\nName: <name>\nElements: <count>\nSum: <value>\nMin: <value>\nMax: <value>\nSample[<idx>]: <value>\n...\nHash: <hex>\n=== END RESULTS ===\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport re\nimport sys\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Optional\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Results:\n    \"\"\"Parsed results from benchmark output.\"\"\"\n    name: str\n    elements: int\n    sum_val: Optional[float]\n    min_val: Optional[float]\n    max_val: Optional[float]\n    samples: dict[int, float]\n    hash_val: str\n    is_integer: bool = False\n\n\ndef parse_results(text: str) -> Optional[Results]:\n    \"\"\"Parse results block from benchmark output.\"\"\"\n    # Integer sample\n            samples[idx] = float(int(val_str))\n    \n    # Determine if integer-based (no Sum field or integer Min/Max)\n    is_integer = sum_match is None\n    \n    sum_val = None\n    min_val = None\n    max_val = None\n    \n    if sum_match:\n        try:\n            sum_val = float(sum_match.group(1).strip())\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n    \n    if min_match:\n        try:\n            min_val = float(min_match.group(1).strip())\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n    \n    if max_match:\n        try:\n            max_val = float(max_match.group(1).strip())\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n    \n    return Results(\n        name=name_match.group(1),\n        elements=int(elements_match.group(1)),\n        sum_val=sum_val,\n        min_val=min_val,\n        max_val=max_val,\n        samples=samples,\n        hash_val=hash_match.group(1).lower(),\n        is_integer=is_integer\n    )\n\n\ndef compare_results(\n    baseline: Results,\n    test: Results,\n    tolerance: float = 1e-10,\n    gpu_tolerance: float = 1e-6,\n    is_gpu: bool = False\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compare test results against baseline.\n    \n    Returns a dict with:\n    - correct: bool\n    - max_rel_error: float\n    - failed_samples: list of dicts with index, expected, actual, rel_error\n    - details: str with human-readable summary\n    \"\"\"\n    result = {\n        'correct': True,\n        'max_rel_error': 0.0,\n        'failed_samples': [],\n        'details': ''\n    }\n    \n    tol = gpu_tolerance if is_gpu else tolerance\n    errors = []\n    \n    # Check element count\n    if baseline.elements != test.elements:\n        result['correct'] = False\n        errors.append(f\"Element count mismatch: expected {baseline.elements}, got {test.elements}\")\n    \n    # For integer data, compare hashes exactly\n    if baseline.is_integer:\n        if baseline.hash_val != test.hash_val:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            errors.append(f\"Hash mismatch: expected {baseline.hash_val}, got {test.hash_val}\")\n        result['details'] = '\\n'.join(errors) if errors else \"Hash match: OK\"\n        return result\n    \n    # For floating-point data, compare samples with tolerance\n    def rel_error(expected: float, actual: float) -> float:\n        if abs(expected) < 1e-15:\n            return abs(actual)\n        return abs(actual - expected) / abs(expected)\n    \n    # Compare sum\n    if baseline.sum_val is not None and test.sum_val is not None:\n        err = rel_error(baseline.sum_val, test.sum_val)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        if err > tol:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            errors.append(f\"Sum mismatch: expected {baseline.sum_val:.17e}, got {test.sum_val:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    # Compare min/max\n    if baseline.min_val is not None and test.min_val is not None:\n        err = rel_error(baseline.min_val, test.min_val)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        if err > tol:\n            errors.append(f\"Min mismatch: expected {baseline.min_val:.17e}, got {test.min_val:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    if baseline.max_val is not None and test.max_val is not None:\n        err = rel_error(baseline.max_val, test.max_val)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        if err > tol:\n            errors.append(f\"Max mismatch: expected {baseline.max_val:.17e}, got {test.max_val:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    # Compare samples\n    for idx in baseline.samples:\n        if idx not in test.samples:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            errors.append(f\"Missing sample at index {idx}\")\n            continue\n        \n        expected = baseline.samples[idx]\n        actual = test.samples[idx]\n        err = rel_error(expected, actual)\n        result['max_rel_error'] = max(result['max_rel_error'], err)\n        \n        if err > tol:\n            result['correct'] = False\n            result['failed_samples'].append({\n                'index': idx,\n                'expected': expected,\n                'actual': actual,\n                'rel_error': err\n            })\n            errors.append(f\"Sample[{idx}] mismatch: expected {expected:.17e}, got {actual:.17e}, rel_error={err:.2e}\")\n    \n    result['details'] = '\\n'.join(errors) if errors else f\"All comparisons passed (max_rel_error={result['max_rel_error']:.2e})\"\n    return result\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Compare benchmark results against baseline')\n    parser.add_argument('baseline', type=Path, help='Baseline output file or stdout capture')\n    parser.add_argument('test', type=Path, help='Test output file or stdout capture')\n    parser.add_argument('--tolerance', '-t', type=float, default=1e-10,\n                        help='Relative tolerance for floating-point comparison (default: 1e-10)')\n    parser.add_argument('--gpu-tolerance', type=float, default=1e-6,\n                        help='Tolerance for GPU results (default: 1e-6)')\n    parser.add_argument('--gpu', action='store_true',\n                        help='Use GPU tolerance (for CUDA/hybrid tiers)')\n    parser.add_argument('--json', action='store_true',\n                        help='Output result as JSON')\n    \n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    \n    # Read files\n    baseline_text = args.baseline.read_text()\n    test_text = args.test.read_text()\n    \n    # Parse results\n    baseline_results = parse_results(baseline_text)\n    test_results = parse_results(test_text)\n    \n    if baseline_results is None:\n        print(\"ERROR: Could not parse baseline results\", file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(2)\n    \n    if test_results is None:\n        print(\"ERROR: Could not parse test results\", file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(2)\n    \n    # Compare\n    comparison = compare_results(\n        baseline_results,\n        test_results,\n        tolerance=args.tolerance,\n        gpu_tolerance=args.gpu_tolerance,\n        is_gpu=args.gpu\n    )\n    \n    if args.json:\n        import json\n","exit":0,"truncated":false},"title":"sed -n '1,240p' /home/petert/llm_eval/py_evaluation/compare_results.py","time":{"start":1792623182143,"end":1792623182179}},"id":"prt_160ab00dd001cmi9ETXRAAWJX6","sessionID":"ses_e9f580e96ffev5OK1hHwVyq9kx","messageID":"msg_160aaf88f001b73HOgBzSVyjZI"}}

Each benchmark is self-contained, builds independently with CMake, and requires only a C++ compiler.\n\n## Benchmarks\n\n### 1. N-Body Simulation (`nbody/`)\nSimulates gravitational interactions between bodies using a direct O(n²) algorithm.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./nbody -n <num_bodies> -s <num_steps> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Number of bodies (default: 1024)\n- `-s`: Number of simulation steps (default: 10)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Verifies that all body positions and velocities remain finite and within reasonable bounds.\n\n### 2. Matrix Multiplication (`matmul/`)\nComputes C = A × B for square matrices using standard triple-nested loop.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./matmul -n <size> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Matrix size N (computes N×N matrices) (default: 512)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Recomputes selected elements and checks for correctness.\n\n### 3. Black-Scholes Option Pricing (`black-scholes/`)\nPrices European call and put options using the Black-Scholes formula.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./black_scholes -n <num_options> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Number of options to price (default: 10000)\n- `-v`: Enable validation against known reference values\n\n**Validation:** Compares computed prices against expected values from standard test cases.\n\n### 4. Floyd-Warshall All-Pairs Shortest Path (`floydwarshall/`)\nComputes shortest paths between all pairs of nodes in a graph.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./floydwarshall -n <num_nodes> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Number of nodes in the graph (default: 512)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Checks diagonal elements are zero, triangle inequality, and symmetry.\n\n### 5. 3D Stencil Computation (`stencil3d/`)\nApplies a 7-point stencil (averaging) operation on a 3D grid iteratively.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./stencil3d -x <size> [-y <size>] [-z <size>] -i <iterations> [-v]\n```\n- `-x`: Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\n- `-y`: Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\n- `-z`: Grid size in Z dimension (default: same as X)\n- `-i`: Number of iterations (default: 10)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Checks for NaN/Inf values and ensures values remain bounded.\n\n### 6. Cholesky Decomposition (`cholesky/`)\nComputes the Cholesky decomposition of a symmetric positive definite matrix.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./cholesky -n <size> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Matrix size (default: 512)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Reconstructs original matrix from L × L^T and checks error.\n\n### 7. Cahn-Hilliard Phase Separation (`cahn-hilliard/`)\nSimulates phase separation dynamics using the Cahn-Hilliard equation.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./cahn_hilliard -x <size> [-y <size>] [-z <size>] -i <iterations> [-v]\n```\n- `-x`: Grid size in X dimension (default: 64)\n- `-y`: Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\n- `-z`: Grid size in Z dimension (default: same as X)\n- `-i`: Number of time steps (default: 20)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Checks for NaN/Inf values and ensures concentration field remains bounded.\n\n### 8. QT Clustering (`qtclustering/`)\nGroups 2D points into clusters using Quality Threshold clustering, ensuring each cluster's diameter stays below a threshold.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./qtclustering -n <num_points> -t <threshold> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Number of points (default: 1000)\n- `-t`: Distance threshold for clustering (default: 2.0)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Verifies that each cluster's diameter is below the threshold and that no point appears in multiple clusters.\n\n### 9. Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication (`spmv/`)\nPerforms sparse matrix-vector multiplication using the Compressed Sparse Row (CSR) format.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./spmv -n <size> -s <sparsity> -i <iterations> [-m <max_val>] [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Number of rows/columns in the matrix (default: 1024)\n- `-s`: Sparsity - 1 out of N entries is non-zero (default: 10, means 90% sparse)\n- `-i`: Number of iterations (default: 10)\n- `-m`: Maximum value for matrix and vector elements (default: 1.0)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Compares results against a reference implementation using relative error checking (2% threshold).\n\n### 10. Unstructured Mesh Simulation (`unstructured/`)\nSimulates energy transfer on an unstructured mesh using a 2D square grid represented as an arbitrary connectivity graph.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./unstructured -n <grid_size> -i <iterations> [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Grid size (NxN elements) (default: 512)\n- `-i`: Number of simulation iterations (default: 10)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Checks that energy sums remain finite and within expected bounds.\n\n### 11. Room Response Simulation (`roomsim/`)\nSimulates room impulse response using radiosity-based wave propagation. Models how sound/light waves propagate between surfaces by computing form factors, simulating wave propagation with time delays, and estimating distances via cross-correlation.\n\n**Usage:**\n```bash\n./roomsim -n <num_triangles> -t <timesteps> [-s <source>] [-r <reflectivity>] [-v]\n```\n- `-n`: Target number of triangles (default: 320, rounded to nearest icosphere level: 20, 80, 320, 1280, 5120, 20480)\n- `-t`: Number of timesteps (default: 50)\n- `-s`: Source triangle index (default: 0)\n- `-r`: Surface reflectivity 0.0-1.0 (default: 0.8)\n- `-v`: Enable validation\n\n**Validation:** Checks that distances are non-negative, some triangles receive energy, and form factors are computed correctly.\n\n## Building\n\n### Build All Benchmarks\n```bash\ncd ~/llm_eval/benchmarks\nmkdir build\ncd build\ncmake ..\nmake\n```\n\n### Build Individual Benchmark\n```bash\ncd ~/llm_eval/benchmarks/<benchmark_name>\nmkdir build\ncd build\ncmake ..\nmake\n```\n\n## Requirements\n\n- CMake 3.10 or higher\n- C++20 compatible compiler\n- Math library (libm)\n\n## Features\n\nAll benchmarks include:\n- **Command-line configuration**: Adjust workload size via command-line arguments\n- **Validation mode**: Optional result verification (-v flag)\n- **Performance metrics**: Timing and throughput measurements\n- **Independent builds**: Each benchmark builds standalone with no external dependencies\n\n## Notes\n\n- These are simplified sequential versions for evaluation purposes\n- Performance is not optimized (no SIMD, threading, or GPU acceleration)\n- Algorithms are straightforward implementations focusing on correctness\n","metadata":{"output":"# Sequential Benchmarks\n\nThis directory contains simplified, purely sequential implementations of benchmarks originally designed for the Celerity parallel computing system. initializeConcentrationCUDA(double* c, const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    dim3 block(8,8,8);\n    dim3 grid((nx+block.x-1)/block.x, (ny+block.y-1)/block.y, (nz+block.z-1)/block.z);\n    initializeConcentrationKernel<<<grid, block>>>(c, nx, ny, nz);\n    cudaDeviceSynchronize();\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Check if values are in reasonable range for concentration field\n    // After Cahn-Hilliard evolution, values should typically remain bounded\n    double minVal = c[0];\n    double maxVal = c[0];\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Concentration range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n    \n    // Values should generally stay within reasonable bounds\n    if (maxVal > 10.0 || minVal < -10.0) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 64)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num> The concentration tile\n// (two-cell halo) is staged in shared memory, the chemical potential is\n// computed into a one-cell-halo shared tile, and the concentration update is\n// then computed from that shared tile. This removes the global-memory\n// round-trip of the mu array and halves the number of kernel launches.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE, 2) void cahnHilliardStepKernel(\n    const double* __restrict__ cold, double* __restrict__ cnew, int nx, int ny, int nz,\n    double invdx2, double invdy2, double invdz2, double gamma, double e_AA, double e_BB,\n    double e_AB, double dtD) {\n    __shared__ double c_tile[C_TILE_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double mu_tile[MU_TILE_SIZE];\n\n    const int bx0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BLOCK_X;\n    const int by0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BLOCK_Y;\n    const int bz0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * BLOCK_Z;\n    const int tid = threadIdx.x + threadIdx.y * BLOCK_X + threadIdx.z * BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y;\n\n    // Stage concentration values including the two-cell halo, applying the\n    // same clamped boundary conditions as the reference code.\n    for (int i = tid; i < C_TILE_SIZE; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int cz = i / C_TILE_XY;\n        const int r = i - cz * C_TILE_XY;\n        const int cy = r / C_TILE_X;\n        const int cx = r - cy * C_TILE_X;\n\n        const int gx = clampIndex(bx0 + cx - 2, nx);\n        const int gy = clampIndex(by0 + cy - 2, ny);\n        const int gz = clampIndex(bz0 + cz - 2, nz);\n        c_tile[i] = cold[(static_cast<size_t>(gz) * ny + gy) * nx + gx];\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    // Compute the chemical potential for the block interior plus a one-cell\n    // halo. For halo positions outside the domain the center is clamped, which\n    // reproduces the reference clamped-boundary semantics.\n    for (int i = tid; i < MU_TILE_SIZE; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int mz = i / MU_TILE_XY;\n        const int r = i - mz * MU_TILE_XY;\n        const int my = r / MU_TILE_X;\n        const int mx = r - my * MU_TILE_X;\n\n        const int cxg = clampIndex(bx0 + mx - 1, nx);\n        const int cyg = clampIndex(by0 + my - 1, ny);\n        const int czg = clampIndex(bz0 + mz - 1, nz);\n\n        const int ccx = cxg - bx0 + 2;\n        const int ccy = cyg - by0 + 2;\n        const int ccz = czg - bz0 + 2;\n        const int ci = (ccz * C_TILE_Y + ccy) * C_TILE_X + ccx;\n\n        const double cv = c_tile[ci];\n        const double cxx = (c_tile[ci + 1] + c_tile[ci - 1] - 2.0 * cv) * invdx2;\n        const double cyy = (c_tile[ci + C_TILE_X] + c_tile[ci - C_TILE_X] - 2.0 * cv) * invdy2;\n        const double czz = (c_tile[ci + C_TILE_XY] + c_tile[ci - C_TILE_XY] - 2.0 * cv) * invdz2;\n\n        mu_tile[i] = 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB) +\n                     3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv - gamma * (cxx + cyy + czz);\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    const int x = bx0 + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = by0 + threadIdx.y;\n    const int z = bz0 + threadIdx.z;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny || z >= nz) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    // The mu tile is indexed so that index (mx,my,mz) corresponds to the\n    // physical cell (bx0+mx-1, by0+my-1, bz0+mz-1). The update thread needs\n    // the cell itself as the stencil center.\n    const int mi = ((threadIdx.z + 1) * MU_TILE_Y + (threadIdx.y + 1)) * MU_TILE_X +\n                   (threadIdx.x + 1);\n    const double muc = mu_tile[mi];\n    const double mxx = (mu_tile[mi + 1] + mu_tile[mi - 1] - 2.0 * muc) * invdx2;\n    const double myy = (mu_tile[mi + MU_TILE_X] + mu_tile[mi - MU_TILE_X] - 2.0 * muc) * invdy2;\n    const double mzz =\n        (mu_tile[mi + MU_TILE_XY] + mu_tile[mi - MU_TILE_XY] - 2.0 * muc) * invdz2;\n\n    const size_t idx = (static_cast<size_t>(z) * ny + y) * nx + x;\n    cnew[idx] = cold[idx] + dtD * (mxx + myy + mzz);\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx,\n                    [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }","lineStart":55,"lineEnd":154,"totalLines":321,"truncated":true}},"title":"tmp/chtest/cahn-hilliard/twok.cpp","time":{"start":1792623417558,"end":1792623417576}},"id":"prt_160ae97e4001ECSr2Xkd6PYCXD","sessionID":"ses_e9f580e96ffev5OK1hHwVyq9kx","messageID":"msg_160ae7c63001imgjX5de2QPgtc"}}

The concentration tile\n// (two-cell halo) is staged in shared memory, the chemical potential is\n// computed into a one-cell-halo shared tile, and the concentration update is\n// then computed from that shared tile. This removes the global-memory\n// round-trip of the mu array and halves the number of kernel launches.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_SIZE, 2) void cahnHilliardStepKernel(\n    const double* __restrict__ cold, double* __restrict__ cnew, int nx, int ny, int nz,\n    double invdx2, double invdy2, double invdz2, double gamma, double e_AA, double e_BB,\n    double e_AB, double dtD) {\n    __shared__ double c_tile[C_TILE_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double mu_tile[MU_TILE_SIZE];\n\n    const int bx0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.x) * BLOCK_X;\n    const int by0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.y) * BLOCK_Y;\n    const int bz0 = static_cast<int>(blockIdx.z) * BLOCK_Z;\n    const int tid = threadIdx.x + threadIdx.y * BLOCK_X + threadIdx.z * BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y;\n\n    // Stage concentration values including the two-cell halo, applying the\n    // same clamped boundary conditions as the reference code.\n    for (int i = tid; i < C_TILE_SIZE; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int cz = i / C_TILE_XY;\n        const int r = i - cz * C_TILE_XY;\n        const int cy = r / C_TILE_X;\n        const int cx = r - cy * C_TILE_X;\n\n        const int gx = clampIndex(bx0 + cx - 2, nx);\n        const int gy = clampIndex(by0 + cy - 2, ny);\n        const int gz = clampIndex(bz0 + cz - 2, nz);\n        c_tile[i] = cold[(static_cast<size_t>(gz) * ny + gy) * nx + gx];\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    // Compute the chemical potential for the block interior plus a one-cell\n    // halo. For halo positions outside the domain the center is clamped, which\n    // reproduces the reference clamped-boundary semantics.\n    for (int i = tid; i < MU_TILE_SIZE; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int mz = i / MU_TILE_XY;\n        const int r = i - mz * MU_TILE_XY;\n        const int my = r / MU_TILE_X;\n        const int mx = r - my * MU_TILE_X;\n\n        const int cxg = clampIndex(bx0 + mx - 1, nx);\n        const int cyg = clampIndex(by0 + my - 1, ny);\n        const int czg = clampIndex(bz0 + mz - 1, nz);\n\n        const int ccx = cxg - bx0 + 2;\n        const int ccy = cyg - by0 + 2;\n        const int ccz = czg - bz0 + 2;\n        const int ci = (ccz * C_TILE_Y + ccy) * C_TILE_X + ccx;\n\n        const double cv = c_tile[ci];\n        const double cxx = (c_tile[ci + 1] + c_tile[ci - 1] - 2.0 * cv) * invdx2;\n        const double cyy = (c_tile[ci + C_TILE_X] + c_tile[ci - C_TILE_X] - 2.0 * cv) * invdy2;\n        const double czz = (c_tile[ci + C_TILE_XY] + c_tile[ci - C_TILE_XY] - 2.0 * cv) * invdz2;\n\n        mu_tile[i] = 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB) +\n                     3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv - gamma * (cxx + cyy + czz);\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    const int x = bx0 + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = by0 + threadIdx.y;\n    const int z = bz0 + threadIdx.z;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny || z >= nz) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    // The mu tile is indexed so that index (mx,my,mz) corresponds to the\n    // physical cell (bx0+mx-1, by0+my-1, bz0+mz-1). start).count();\n\n    printf(\"Computation time: %.3f ms\\n\", elapsedMs);\n\n    // Calculate performance\n    double cellUpdates = (double)gridSize * iterations;\n    double mcups = elapsedMs > 0.0 ? cellUpdates / (elapsedMs / 1000.0) / 1e6 : 0.0;\n    printf(\"Performance: %.3f MCellUpdates/s\\n\", mcups);\n\n    // Only transfer the final field back when it is actually observed.\n    std::vector<double> hostCold;\n    if (printResults || validate) {\n        hostCold.resize(gridSize);\n        CUDA_CHECK(\n            cudaMemcpy(hostCold.data(), d_c, gridSize * sizeof(double), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost));\n    }\n\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_c));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_mu));\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        print_results(hostCold, \"Concentration\");\n    }\n\n    // Validation\n    if (validate) {\n        printf(\"Validating result...\\n\");\n        bool valid = validateResult(hostCold, nx, ny, nz);\n\n        if (valid) {\n            printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return 0;\n}","lineStart":210,"lineEnd":319,"totalLines":319,"truncated":false}},"title":"home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cahn-hilliard_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_cuda_r4/cahn-hilliard/cahn_hilliard.cpp","time":{"start":1792625011998,"end":1792625012016}},"id":"prt_160c6ec38001wzp2Rb0emUauC1","sessionID":"ses_e9f580e96ffev5OK1hHwVyq9kx","messageID":"msg_160c6dec0001Z8UqXt8MGPoIp1"}}
